Paris, Blois, Tours, Moulins → Rome · homophonic nomenclator, variable-length figures run together · 1587, August 1588 – July 1589

Cardinal Morosini, nuncio and legate in France, to Rome, 1587–89

→

Archivio Apostolico Vaticano, Segreteria di Stato, Francia 22, items 1–31 and 34–37 · DECODE R18–R22, R27–R49, R51, R52, R55–R58, thirty-four cipher enclosures; and Francia 18/3, DECODE R17 · catalogued as partially decrypted

The 1587 key printed by Meister, which Lasry identified from his statistical recovery of part of it, was applied to DECODE’s transcriptions. The figures run together in units of one to three digits, so the key cannot be applied mechanically: the division into units, and with it the decryption, was chosen with a language model, then every break re-parsed by hand on the figures · all 35 deciphered with the known key into sentences and translated, with about 540 short holes in 33,000 words still to check on the images: partial · no plaintext had been posted

Daniel Bourdeau · posted 19 September 2026 · updated 22 October 2026

Method: not solved · Extent: text not obtained

Credits · George Lasry (key or decipherment used) · Aloys Meister (key or decipherment used)

not solved
On this pageThe documents and the keyThe murder of the GuisesAll thirty-five, in briefMethodWhat remains uncertainFull textsSources
Summary. Gian Francesco Morosini, Bishop of Brescia, was nuncio in France from 1587 and legate from the summer of 1588. His despatches to the Cardinal Nephew, Alessandro Peretti di Montalto, sent their substance in enclosed sheets of figures. Thirty-six of these sheets, from Francia 22 in the Vatican, are on DECODE, transcribed digit by digit, with George Lasry’s partial key of 2020, which he recovered statistically, and a scan of the full key Aloys Meister printed in 1906 (“Con monsig. Moresino nuntio in Francia, 8 Giugno 1587”), which Lasry identified as the same key. No plaintext was posted, and the records are listed as partially decrypted. The key cannot be applied mechanically: the figures run on without separators and the homophones are one, two or three digits long (e is 35, 153 or 5), so a sheet can be divided into units in many ways. A search over the divisions, scored by a language model of 16th-century Italian, gives a run of Italian words. Thirty-four sheets were decoded end to end, and so was a despatch of 1587 filed in another volume, Francia 18 (R17). Two sheets from the spring of 1589 are in a second, syllabic key that Lasry recovered only in part: a different key, with its own page. What they say: the long fight to have the Council of Trent published in France; the Estates of Blois; the Duke of Savoy’s seizure of Saluzzo; the Duke of Guise “amazato a colpi di pugnale” in the King’s chamber on 23 December 1588, and his brother the Cardinal the next morning; the legate’s own disgrace in Rome; and, from Tours and Moulins, Henri III’s march on Paris in June 1589. Where it stands. The first decode carried garbled words in nearly every line, and the reading audit of 22 October 2026 set all 35 back to decoded, not read. The same day every break was re-parsed by hand on the figures, with image crops where a figure was in doubt: most garbles were spurious nomenclator dots in the volunteer transcriptions and the clerk’s -mente abbreviation (m and the null 8). All 35 enclosures now read as sentences and are translated (the last four, R27, R35, R51 and R57, on 23 October), so the text is deciphered with the known key throughout. The entry stays partial because about 540 short holes in 33,000 words (openings, closings, one-to-eight-figure groups no word parse fits) have been checked on the images only for R49, R51 and R55 and parts of R27, R35, R39, R40 and R57. On R40 two holes turned out to be stretches crossed through by the encipherer, which DECODE transcribes as figures. Open besides: ten codes, and the stretches of R35 lost to a hole in the paper.

01 The documents and the key

Francia 22 is the volume of Morosini’s originals and ciphers from 25 July 1588 to 20 September 1589 (ff. 10–583). DECODE holds 36 of its cipher enclosures, items 22/1 to 22/37 (22/29 has no record; R23–R26 and R50, inside the same run of record numbers, are other material). Most are marked dup.to, the duplicate sent by a second courier; the clear covering letters that go with them are partly transcribed on DECODE as well. Two of them, R53 and R54 (items 22/32–33), are in a second key and have their own page; the other thirty-four are in the key below.

A thirty-fifth despatch in the same key sits in another volume. DECODE R17 (Francia 18/3, ff. 233r–235v) is filed with the despatches of the bishop of Bergamo and the archbishop of Nazareth of 1585–86, with an incomplete key note (“ASV F22”) that points to Meister no. 40 and a “decryption” that is the error trace of a Java program. The decoder below reads it on all five pages, so it is a despatch of Morosini’s nunciature, from 1587 (it moved to this page from the Francia 18 page on 14 October 2026).

The key is Meister no. 40 (Die Geheimschrift im Dienste der päpstlichen Kurie, pp. 393–394), issued to Morosini on 8 June 1587. Each letter has two or three homophones: a 21 212 2, e 35 153 5, i 17 171 7, n 10 01 0, o 91 191 9, r 00 13 1, s 54 45 4, t 51 15 6, u 37 173 3, and so on. There are about twenty codes for clusters (cr 22, pr 33, st 47, str 49) and twenty for words (che 56, per 64, non 61 71, quando 99). A three-digit nomenclator is marked with a dot on the middle figure: 111 the Pope, 113 the King of France (“S. M.tà Christ.ma”), 116 the Queen Mother, 129 Duca di, 131 Cardinale di, 133 Arcivescovo, 227 Heretici, 232 “il già Re di Navarra”. An 8 with any following figure is a null.

02 The murder of the Guises

The despatch of 23 December 1588 (R43) begins, in the reading of 22 October 2026:

[…] questa matina di […] il duca di Guisa nela camera del Re è stato per ordine di S. M.tà amazato a colpi di pugnale, et la sua morte è seguita a questo modo. […] mandato a chiamare il duca di Guisa, che si truouaua nel Consiglio di Stato, uenuto et entrato subito nela camera fu preso da due a trauerso et da altri aterato con molte pugnalate, et dopo hauere indarno fata grandisima difesa con le mani et con i denti, senza però mai hauere potuto caciare mano ala spada. […] Morto lui, et posti prigioni il cardinale di Guisa, che si truouaua pure in Consiglio, il duca di Nemurs, il duca d’Elbeuf, il principe di Ianuile, l’arciuescouo Lione, il preuosto di mercanti di Parigi et il presidente Nogli, et mesa guardia al cardinale di Borbone, il Re andò a ritrouare la Regina Madre

(This morning the Duke of Guise was killed with daggers in His Majesty’s chamber on His Majesty’s order. Called from the council of state, he came in and was seized, and, after a great defence with his hands and teeth, without ever getting his hand to his sword, died of many dagger blows. The Cardinal de Guise, Nemours, Elbeuf, Joinville, the Archbishop of Lyon, the prévôt des marchands of Paris and the président Nogli were arrested, and the Cardinal de Bourbon put under guard.) The duplicate of the next day (R44) adds that the Cardinal de Guise “questa matina solam[ente] è rimasto priuo di uita”. The legate, because a cardinal had been killed, leaves the answer to the Pope.

03 All thirty-five, in brief

Dates in square brackets come from the context or the series. The quotations are from the readings of 22–23 October 2026 (texts/<R>_reading.md), in the clerk’s spelling (u for v, single for double consonants), with [?word] for a word the figures carry only in part. The summaries were first written from the September decode and were checked against the new readings where they differed (R39: it is Guise, not Nevers, who presses for war; R40: the levy is 15,000 Swiss and 8,000 landsknechts). Each reading file has its English translation (_en.md).

RecordItemDateWhat the cipher saysFrom the text
R17Francia 18/3[1587]The German reiter army and the Catholic princes. All good men had rejoiced at the King's resolve to call the Duke of Lorraine and the Duke of Guise to join their forces with his, and the change of mind that followed has caused still greater dismay. The Queen Mother is in great distress, fearing an irreparable mishap; the Duke of Lorraine is urged to come forward. Schomberg is named.[?la] Regina Madre sta con molto trauaglio, temendo [?che] non segua qualche inconueniente iremediabile
R1822/110 Aug [1588] (duplicate)On publishing the Council of Trent: strong resistance everywhere over Gallican liberties. In Paris Morosini lobbied the Sorbonne and the royal council, and told Henri III that a Most Christian king should accept the council without conditions. The King answered that the clergy, above all the Archbishop of Lyon, were the ones opposing it. Also: the King is set on holding the Estates General; Guise's party is manoeuvring over the deputies; the Spanish ambassador complains that the English fleet was resupplied in French ports.si contentase anco di riceuere nel suo regno eso concilio liberam[ente], come si è fato neli altri regni
R1922/224 Aug [1588] (duplicate)The clergy's deputies appeared before the Parlement to oppose the council as against Gallican liberty. In an audience the King defended his privileges and benefice nominations. He was told that the legation's faculties must be verified by the Parlement. A Cardinal (Bourbon/Vendôme) pressed for the absolution of his brothers. Factions are gathering followers for the Estates; there is fear of a bloody clash between Catholics; a marriage is proposed between the Count of Soissons and a daughter of the Duke of Guise.comparuero al parlamento per oponersi a la publicatione di eso concilio, dicendo che era contrario in molte cose ala libertà dela chiesa galicana
R2022/39 Sept [1588]The King gave the Count of Soissons letters clearing him of his service with Navarre and the Huguenots. Paris citizens and the prévôt protested to the Parlement. Cardinal de Vendôme and the Princess of Condé were furious. Morosini advised the King and Vendôme to wait for the Pope's absolution first, and they agreed to hold the letters back until it came.le lettere non hauesero a seruire se non dopo che al conte fose data la asolutione
R2122/4n.d. [Sept 1588]The King dismissed his secretaries of state and the chancellor all at once, leaving the court anxious. Mons. de Nevers(?) and the Queen Mother were displeased not to have been consulted. Morosini warned the King that new men would be inexperienced and the old ones could side with his enemies. The King replied that he meant to be obeyed and feared. Also: the King sent his physician to Épernon to get him to give up Boulogne and Metz.priuandosi S. M.tà in un isteso tempo di tuti quelli che intendono il uero stato dele cose sue
R2222/5n.d. [Sept 1588] (duplicate)Replies to Montalto. Morosini has kept pressing for the council; the Estates promise to ask for it. His faculties will not be obeyed unless the Parlement verifies them. He held back the Count of Soissons' absolution because the Count refused the prescribed form of abjuration; the Cardinals of Bourbon and Vendôme then urged the King to write to Rome. Also: a Danish ambassador has come, perhaps to draw the King into defending the Queen of England; Morosini rebuts the vice-legate's complaints and answers on Épernon and La Valette.non si deue acusare di pecato che non ha pure pensato, non che comeso
R2722/6n.d. (fragment)On governments for Épernon (Saintonge, Angoulême, Cognac) and on Boulogne. Épernon's side refuses to give anything back and is said to deal with the Queen of England and Navarre. Morosini hopes for peace between Guise and the others, though with little confidence.si mostrano risolutisimi di non uolere restituire alcuna cosa
R2822/7[early] Oct [1588] (duplicate)While walking in the palace garden with the King, the Queen Mother and the Cardinals of Bourbon and Vendôme, Morosini learned that the King would send Signor Girolamo Gondi to Rome about pulling Marshal Montmorency away from the Huguenots. Morosini urged the King to win Guise over with money, since Guise is heavily in debt; Guise refused the grant offered. Vendôme reports that Cardinal Joyeuse worked against the Count of Soissons in Rome.esortata S. M.tà a fare ogni opera per ritirare dal'amicitia de li ugonotti quel signore
R2922/810 Oct [1588] (duplicate)A messenger is back from Florence about the Grand Duke's marriage to the Princess of Lorraine; Orazio Rucellai will conclude it, and the Queen Mother plans to escort her to Marseille. News that the Duke of Savoy has seized the whole marquisate of Saluzzo; Guise fears this will force peace with Navarre and the heretics. Morosini asks for orders; a Danish ambassador is dealing over merchants' goods.questo acidente sarà la rouina dela religione catolica
R3022/93 Oct [1588], BloisMorosini took on the job of reconciling the Duke of Guise and Mons. de Nevers, who have been at odds since they married sisters; with the King's approval, both promised to follow his advice. He pressed Guise and the Cardinal of Guise on publishing the council. The clergy is split: the lower deputies want Gallican liberty reserved. He is also working against any alienation of church property.era causa che così l'uno come l'altro non [?lasciaua] di fare di mali ofici con il Re, a graue pregiudicio dela quiete
R3122/1023 Oct [1588], BloisMandelot, governor of Lyon, is ordered to arrest a man for transfer to Avignon. The Cardinal of Guise asks for the vice-legation of Avignon. The Florentine marriage contract is going forward. The King will send Gondi to Rome about Montmorency, and is embittered over Savoy and Saluzzo. The Épernon and Provence troubles continue. The Estates have sworn a fundamental law keeping only the Catholic religion; the Parlement will not accept the legation faculties.per lege fondamentale del regno et ireuocabile si è stabilito di mantenere sempre la sola religione catolica
R3222/1124 Oct [1588]Savoy is keeping Saluzzo and is suspected of designs on Marseille. M. de Poigny is sent to the Duke to demand restitution. Morosini protests over Cardinal Lenoncourt's indult and over church revenues in the Cambrai truce, and the Queen Mother answers him. The King explains why Épernon was removed from Provence, and promises to raise publication of the council "tomorrow".Ho anco di nuouo ricordato a S. M.tà la publicatione del concilio di Trento
R3322/12n.d. [Oct-Nov 1588]The King referred the council to the clergy's congregation. There the chapters, bishops and others quarrelled over exemptions, and they finally asked for publication with four reservations (royal authority, Gallican liberty, chapter exemptions, particular privileges). Morosini argued that it must be published without conditions. The King answered that the Gallican church has privileges it cannot lose. Morosini asks Rome for precise orders.in ogni modo si doueua fare senza alcuna ecetione
R3422/13n.d. [Nov 1588]The Spanish ambassador pressed the Queen Mother for union and good understanding between Philip II and Henri III, and showed a copy of Philip's letter. Morosini sees a real opening for the alliance and asks that it be kept secret. The ambassador blamed Savoy for the Saluzzo attack. Gondi leaves for Rome; Morosini commends the Archbishop of Lyon. There is new friction with Guise over the governor of Orléans.introdure una buona inteligenza et unione fra il suo et questo Re
R3522/14n.d.A copy, in Spanish, of Philip II's letter to the Queen Mother (given to her by the Spanish ambassador). It urges closer union and friendship between the two crowns against the heretics, for God's service and obedience to the Roman Church.exortando a las dos coronas a maio[r] union i amistad
R3622/1510 Nov [1588] (duplicate)A fever kept Morosini indoors. The Savoy ambassador reported the Poigny talks to the Queen Mother: the Duke will not return the Saluzzo places, and claims to have acted by the Pope's order (Carmagnola). Morosini firmly denies that the Pope had any part in it. The King is resolved on war rather than bear the insult; Morosini asks for guidance.tuto quello che ha fato è di ordine di N. S.
R3722/1621 Nov [1588] (duplicate)Morosini urged the King and Queen Mother to settle Saluzzo peacefully. The Savoy ambassador had offered to restore the places if they were given to a Catholic knight, with Nemours as governor. Guise advised force instead, sending Mayenne into Bresse. The King inclined to Morosini's view and asked that a lieutenant also be named. The Spanish ambassador denies that Philip backs Savoy.il duca di Guisa consigliaua il contrario … bisognaua atendere ad ingrosare le forze al duca di Umena
R3822/17n.d. [late Nov-Dec 1588]The Savoy ambassador complained that Mayenne was ordered into Bresse. Morosini urged the King not to start a war before trying every other way, and to fight the heretics first. The King said he gave Mayenne no such order, but called the Duke of Savoy ungrateful. Also: the Cardinal of Guise and the oath for the Avignon legation; Lenoncourt's indult; a truce with the Dauphiné Huguenots is being considered.esortandola a non uolere principiare una guerra se prima non hauese tenta[t]o ogn'altra uia
R3922/188 Dec [1588] (duplicate)A courier from Turin came from Poigny. In the council, Guise insisted on war with Savoy even if Saluzzo were restored; the others would forgive the Duke if he gave the places back. Munitions and artillery are to be sent to Lyon. Guise also argued that Spain would not intervene.quando anco il sig. duca di Sauoia restituise il marchesato … egli con la sola restitutione non sodisfaceua al'ofesa fata a questa corona
R4022/19dup., "li 8 X[bre]" (8 Dec 1588?), [Blois]The King's council discusses the Duke of Savoy's artillery and troops on the borders of Bresse and Dauphiné and resolves to levy 15,000 Swiss, 8,000 landsknechts and 1,500 reiters to join the Duke of Mayenne; the Duke of Guise offers 10,000 scudi, and the Estates' treasurers promise money. Morosini goes to the Capuchins hoping to find Henri III and complains that the King no longer talks openly with him. He reports that the King is tightly bound to Guise and is said to have dismissed his heads of finance.fu risoluto di spedire subito per fare una leuata di 15 milia Suizeri [et] di oto milia Lanzi
R4122/20n.d. [Blois, Dec 1588]The Duke of Savoy has seized the Marquisate of Saluzzo and offers to give it back once the Dauphiné is free of Huguenots, with his wife or a son as a hostage. The Duke of Guise tells Morosini he must press the King to recover Saluzzo by force, or else retire from court; the Savoyard ambassador has been refused. Mayenne asks what to do, with the Savoyard enemy so near.egli non può per honor suo lasare di far ogni sforzo per uendicare l'ingiuria
R4222/21n.d. [Blois, Dec 1588]Morosini tells the King at the Capuchins that the Pope is displeased the Council of Trent has not been published; the King answers through Gondi. Guise complains that the King does not trust him, and Morosini urges the King to satisfy Guise. He also reports on the papal bull against procurers of abortion and says the Queen Mother has a fever.nela publicatione del concilio di Trento S. M.tà non habia ancora presa quella risolutione
R4322/22Blois, 23 Dec [1588]Guise is killed with daggers in the King's chamber on the King's order, after warnings that he meant harm to the King. The Cardinal of Guise, the Prince de Joinville, the Dukes of Nemours and Elbeuf, the Archbishop of Lyon, the prévôt des marchands and a Paris president are arrested, and the Cardinal de Bourbon is put under guard. The Queen Mother is left speechless, the city is locked down, Morosini seeks an audience in vain, and the Cardinal of Guise is feared dead too.il duca di Guisa nela camera del Re è stato per ordine di S. M.tà amazato a colpi di pugnale
R4422/23dup., 24 Xbre [1588], [Blois]The Cardinal of Guise was put to death only this morning. Because he was a churchman, Morosini leaves the response to the Pope's judgment. The Cardinal de Bourbon is under guard and ill, and Joinville and the Archbishop of Lyon are to go to Amboise. Guise's secretary, under examination, said the Pope had written to Guise in favour of Savoy over Saluzzo, which angered the King. The King tells the legate he is now king and must be obeyed.questa matina solam[ente] è rimasto priuo di uita
R4522/24n.d. [Blois, Jan 1589, after Catherine de' Medici's death]Morosini defends his conduct. The Archbishop of Lyon refuses lay examiners because he is primate of France. The Estates' article excluding Navarre carries a clause reserving the Bourbon succession. Morosini offers condolences on the Queen Mother's death and presses the King to declare Navarre a heretic and incapable, since an envoy of Navarre is at court seeking an accord. He also urges publication of Trent without conditions.dechiarare il Nauarro per heretico et incapace ala sucesione di questo regno
R4622/25Blois, 24 Jan 1589 (dup.)The Pope rejects the clause "saluis iuribus regis et regni", and Morosini works on the King's confessor and the Keeper of the Seals, who promise to argue his case. Saluzzo is set aside for now. At length Morosini complains of the Pope's displeasure and the false reports reaching Rome, and he begs leave to come to Rome to justify himself.non solo non aceta la clausula "saluis iuribus regis et regni"
R4722/26n.d. [Blois, Jan 1589]Morosini continues his defence and denies ever dealing with the English ambassador. He explains why the Council could not be forced through the parlements and the Estates. He had suggested that the King publish it at once and then send a personage to Rome to ask for his privileges.l'ambasciatore d'Inghilterra, il quale né conosco né so mai di hauere ueduto
R4822/27Blois, 30 & 31 Jan 1589Morosini again asks for his leave. The Parisians have put themselves under Mayenne's protection, and the King is leaving for Amboise with his prisoners. Morosini protests about moving the Cardinal de Bourbon and the Archbishop of Lyon. The King says he will keep the Cardinal comfortably in a fine castle, and agrees to send letters ordering all bishops to publish the Council of Trent without conditions.che lei acetaua il sodeto concilio di Trento, [?et] che loro lo facesero publicare
R4922/28n.d. (fragment) [early 1589]The Archbishop of Lyon refuses to answer the royal commissioners or a bishop, saying he is primate of France. His relatives ask Morosini to plead with the King not to prejudice ecclesiastical dignity and to refer the case to the Pope.egli era primate di Francia et che non poteua esere esaminato
R5122/30Tours, 27 March 1589A count's gentleman brings news that two cavalry companies of Mayenne's (under Sagona and Farlandra, who was badly wounded) have been routed and their baggage taken. Orléans and Paris are in the League's hands, and Mayenne remains in Paris. The King wants peace, while Navarre is taking places. Guards have been placed on Morosini's house.S. M.tà desidera grandem. la pace
R5222/31Tours, 6 April 1589The castellan of Angers refuses the League's bribe, and Marshal d'Aumont secures the castle for the King, who writes him a letter in his own hand. The count is sent to Brittany against the Duke of Mercoeur; Alfonso Corso is in Auvergne; Épernon's command is disputed; Mayenne is still in Paris. The King keeps Holy Week and Easter devotions and touches for the scrofula.il castelo, che è riputato il più forte di Francia
R53, R5422/32–33Tours, spring 1589; Moulins, 11 May 1589In a second, syllabic key (DECODE “F22b”) that Lasry reconstructed in part: see their own page.
R5522/34Moulins, 11 June 1589A gentleman of the Grand Duchess of Tuscany reports that the Comte de Soissons (?) was captured in bed by the Duke of Mercoeur, and that a League count was killed by a gunpowder explosion. The King is marching on Paris, where Mayenne is and the city is barricaded, with 4,000 horse and 17,000 foot; Swiss and Casimir's cavalry are coming, and Épernon has taken Montereau. Morosini also passes on, with doubts, a rumour of a plot to kill the King.esendo nel leto et dormendo fu fato prigione dal duca di Mercurio
R5622/35Moulins, 16(?) June 1589Morosini is distressed by Montalto's letter about his secretary. The monitorium has reportedly been posted at St Peter's, and Cardinal Joyeuse and the French ambassador are leaving Rome. Morosini thinks of retiring to Lyon, but the Lyonnais will let him stay only three days. Moulins has sworn loyalty to the King before M. de la Guiche; the Archbishop of Lyon may be freed; Alfonso Corso has crossed the Rhône.pensauo di ritirarmi a Lione per metermi in sicuro
R5722/36n.d. (enclosures dated "dix di giugno" [1589], Moulins)This record holds copies of papers sent to M. de la Guiche. The first says the King's Swiss army (about 12,000 Swiss and 2,000 lansquenets, some 17–18,000 men with 12 guns) is marching, paid with money lent by Strasbourg. The second is the oath of the inhabitants of Moulins to live and die Catholic and loyal to "Henri, by the grace of God King of France and Poland".prestato il giuramento di fedeltà al Re nostro sire Henrico
R5822/37Moulins, 5 July 1589 (dup.)The King has taken Jargeau ("Giorgio") and its Loire bridge, hanged a captain and pardoned the governor. Other Loire towns have surrendered, and the King is heading for Paris, where four faubourgs are said to favour him. Mayenne has taken Montereau and Nemours is raising money at Lyon for the League's Swiss. The count is said to have escaped from Nantes castle in a basket, which Morosini doubts.Si è inteso che il Re habia preso Giorgio

04 Method

DECODE’s transcriptions give the figures one by one, marking a dotted figure X Y^. Z. Dotted groups were taken as nomenclator codes. The rest of each page is a single string of digits, and each position can open a code of one, two or three figures. The decoder (targets/morosini1588/decrypt.py) keeps, at every position, the best-scoring decryption for each four-letter context. It scores the letters it emits with the repository’s shared Italian 5-gram model (lang/, it-cinquecento, built from Renaissance letters and nunciature reports, without spaces). It charges a fixed cost for a null (8 and any figure) and a larger one for a figure it cannot place. A page of 1,500 figures runs in about a second. The Spanish enclosure (R35, a copy of Philip II’s letter to the Queen Mother) comes out in readable Spanish although the model is Italian. R17 went through the same decoder unchanged, from DECODE’s transcription D1611 (volunteer TimB, 2015).

The re-reading of 22–23 October 2026. The character decode above follows the gist but does not build sentences. A word-level decoder with a model of transcription slips (research/reading_audit/reread_b00_decode.py) split each page into words; every break was then re-parsed by hand on the transcribed figures and, where a figure was in doubt, on crops of the DECODE images (55 corrections from the images). Each reading was aligned word by word with its figures, and every word the figures do not fully carry is bracketed. What had garbled the decode: spurious nomenclator dots in DECODE’s transcriptions (250 is “Bles” in letters), real codes with the dot on the wrong figure or none, the clerk’s -mente abbreviation (m and the null 8), numbers in plain figures between nulls, and page order (R30, R31, R43, R45, R47). No new code value came out of it. Tempesti’s Vita di Sisto V (1754), which paraphrases many of these letters, was used for names and sense only, never as a source of words. The readings are texts/<R>_reading.md, with English in _en.md and the corrections and logs beside them.

05 What remains uncertain

  • Nomenclator: 113 is “Il Re di Francia, S. M.tà Christ.ma” in Meister and is rendered “S. M.tà” throughout (H). 165 and 166, printed “quelli” and “questi”, serve for every form of quello and questo (H), and 161 likewise for quanto (C).
  • Dotted groups not in Meister’s table (162, 191, 250, 516, 617, 712, 716, 770 and a few others): the re-reading of 22 October 2026 showed them to be spurious dots on ordinary letter groups, or real codes with the dot on the wrong figure. No new code value.
  • About 540 short holes in 33,000 words of reading: openings and closings, and groups of one to eight figures that no word parse fits. On 23 October 2026 every hole of R49, R51 and R55 was checked on the images, with sample lines of R27, R35, R39, R40 and R57; many are encipherer’s slips transcribed as written, and on R40 (f. 293r ll. 15 and 21) two are stretches the encipherer crossed through, which DECODE gives as ordinary figures. The other records are still to be cropped (listed in each texts/<R>_reread_log.md). R35 loses a stretch to a hole in the paper (f. 202v l. 8) and its last line to damage.
  • Letter-level slips in the first decode (“euisone” for Soissons) are corrected in the readings; the raw decode stays in decrypt_raw.txt. Proper names are graded C (Soissons, Nevers, Poigny); Tempesti’s paraphrase confirms several.
  • Rome’s own decipherments of these despatches are in Segreteria di Stato, Francia 27 (Hübner, Sixte-Quint, 1870, cites them as “déchiffré à Rome, 9 janvier 1589”), not imaged online. Missing leaves: R46 between ff. 382v and 383r, R47 after f. 385v; R17 f. 233v and the last leaf of R22 are not imaged.
  • R17: five groups open (519, 911, 556, 722, 561, most likely transcription slips), and the date within 1587 is not fixed.
  • Item 22/29 is not on DECODE and was not seen. The clear covering letters were not edited here.

Full texts

The transcription and the decryption of every document on this page, so that the reading can be used and checked. Short files are shown here; all are in the repository.

R18, Francia 22/1, 10 Aug [1588] (duplicate) decoded, not read

On publishing the Council of Trent: strong resistance everywhere over Gallican liberties. In Paris Morosini lobbied the Sorbonne and the royal council, and told Henri III that a Most Christian king should accept the council without conditions. The King answered that the clergy, above all the Archbishop of Lyon, were the ones opposing it.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

R29, Francia 22/8, 10 Oct [1588] (duplicate) decoded, not read

A messenger is back from Florence about the Grand Duke's marriage to the Princess of Lorraine; Orazio Rucellai will conclude it, and the Queen Mother plans to escort her to Marseille. News that the Duke of Savoy has seized the whole marquisate of Saluzzo; Guise fears this will force peace with Navarre and the heretics. Morosini asks for orders; a Danish ambassador is dealing over merchants' goods.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

R30, Francia 22/9, 3 Oct [1588], Blois decoded, not read

Morosini took on the job of reconciling the Duke of Guise and Mons. de Nevers, who have been at odds since they married sisters; with the King's approval, both promised to follow his advice. He pressed Guise and the Cardinal of Guise on publishing the council. The clergy is split: the lower deputies want Gallican liberty reserved.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

R32, Francia 22/11, 24 Oct [1588] decoded, not read

Savoy is keeping Saluzzo and is suspected of designs on Marseille. M. de Poigny is sent to the Duke to demand restitution. Morosini protests over Cardinal Lenoncourt's indult and over church revenues in the Cambrai truce, and the Queen Mother answers him.

R33, Francia 22/12, n.d. [Oct-Nov 1588] decoded, not read

The King referred the council to the clergy's congregation. There the chapters, bishops and others quarrelled over exemptions, and they finally asked for publication with four reservations (royal authority, Gallican liberty, chapter exemptions, particular privileges). Morosini argued that it must be published without conditions.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

R34, Francia 22/13, n.d. [Nov 1588] decoded, not read

The Spanish ambassador pressed the Queen Mother for union and good understanding between Philip II and Henri III, and showed a copy of Philip's letter. Morosini sees a real opening for the alliance and asks that it be kept secret. The ambassador blamed Savoy for the Saluzzo attack.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

R35, Francia 22/14, n.d. decoded, not read

A copy, in Spanish, of Philip II's letter to the Queen Mother (given to her by the Spanish ambassador). It urges closer union and friendship between the two crowns against the heretics, for God's service and obedience to the Roman Church.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

R36, Francia 22/15, 10 Nov [1588] (duplicate) decoded, not read

A fever kept Morosini indoors. The Savoy ambassador reported the Poigny talks to the Queen Mother: the Duke will not return the Saluzzo places, and claims to have acted by the Pope's order (Carmagnola). Morosini firmly denies that the Pope had any part in it.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

R37, Francia 22/16, 21 Nov [1588] (duplicate) decoded, not read

Morosini urged the King and Queen Mother to settle Saluzzo peacefully. The Savoy ambassador had offered to restore the places if they were given to a Catholic knight, with Nemours as governor. Guise advised force instead, sending Mayenne into Bresse.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

R38, Francia 22/17, n.d. [late Nov-Dec 1588] decoded, not read

The Savoy ambassador complained that Mayenne was ordered into Bresse. Morosini urged the King not to start a war before trying every other way, and to fight the heretics first. The King said he gave Mayenne no such order, but called the Duke of Savoy ungrateful.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

Transcription: DOC_R38_D1702_1702.txt

R40, Francia 22/19, dup., "li 8 X[bre]" (8 Dec 1588?), [Blois] decoded, not read

The King's council discusses the Duke of Savoy's artillery and troops on the borders of Bresse and Dauphiné and resolves to levy 15,000 Swiss, 8,000 landsknechts and 1,500 reiters to join the Duke of Mayenne; the Duke of Guise offers 10,000 scudi, and the Estates' treasurers promise money. Morosini goes to the Capuchins hoping to find Henri III and complains that the King no longer talks openly with him. He reports that the King is tightly bound to Guise and is said to have dismissed his heads of finance.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

R41, Francia 22/20, n.d. [Blois, Dec 1588] decoded, not read

The Duke of Savoy has seized the Marquisate of Saluzzo and offers to give it back once the Dauphiné is free of Huguenots, with his wife or a son as a hostage. The Duke of Guise tells Morosini he must press the King to recover Saluzzo by force, or else retire from court; the Savoyard ambassador has been refused. Mayenne asks what to do, with the Savoyard enemy so near.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

R42, Francia 22/21, n.d. [Blois, Dec 1588] decoded, not read

Morosini tells the King at the Capuchins that the Pope is displeased the Council of Trent has not been published; the King answers through Gondi. Guise complains that the King does not trust him, and Morosini urges the King to satisfy Guise. He also reports on the papal bull against procurers of abortion and says the Queen Mother has a fever.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

R43, Francia 22/22, Blois, 23 Dec [1588] decoded, not read

Guise is killed with daggers in the King's chamber on the King's order, after warnings that he meant harm to the King. The Cardinal of Guise, the Prince de Joinville, the Dukes of Nemours and Elbeuf, the Archbishop of Lyon, the prévôt des marchands and a Paris president are arrested, and the Cardinal de Bourbon is put under guard. The Queen Mother is left speechless, the city is locked down, Morosini seeks an audience in vain, and the Cardinal of Guise is feared dead too.

R45, Francia 22/24, n.d. [Blois, Jan 1589, after Catherine de' Medici's death] decoded, not read

Morosini defends his conduct. The Archbishop of Lyon refuses lay examiners because he is primate of France. The Estates' article excluding Navarre carries a clause reserving the Bourbon succession.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

R46, Francia 22/25, Blois, 24 Jan 1589 (dup.) decoded, not read

The Pope rejects the clause "saluis iuribus regis et regni", and Morosini works on the King's confessor and the Keeper of the Seals, who promise to argue his case. Saluzzo is set aside for now. At length Morosini complains of the Pope's displeasure and the false reports reaching Rome, and he begs leave to come to Rome to justify himself.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

Transcription: DOC_R46_D1711_1711.txt

R47, Francia 22/26, n.d. [Blois, Jan 1589] decoded, not read

Morosini continues his defence and denies ever dealing with the English ambassador. He explains why the Council could not be forced through the parlements and the Estates. He had suggested that the King publish it at once and then send a personage to Rome to ask for his privileges.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

R48, Francia 22/27, Blois, 30 & 31 Jan 1589 decoded, not read

Morosini again asks for his leave. The Parisians have put themselves under Mayenne's protection, and the King is leaving for Amboise with his prisoners. Morosini protests about moving the Cardinal de Bourbon and the Archbishop of Lyon.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

Transcription: DOC_R48_D1713_1713.txt

R49, Francia 22/28, n.d. (fragment) [early 1589] decoded, not read

The Archbishop of Lyon refuses to answer the royal commissioners or a bishop, saying he is primate of France. His relatives ask Morosini to plead with the King not to prejudice ecclesiastical dignity and to refer the case to the Pope.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

quando non siano pagati procurarano dio cui pare qualche citaro paese di sua maesta ale non sapensato di
rimediare consnla signamenti gia scriti uti questo rumori seruono a la grandeza del il gia re di nauarra
etdeli heretici et molto piu se sucede se qualche inconueniente ne la persona del re haueua sua maesta
comandato et poi che l arciuescouo dilio ne ricusaua di rispondere alico mi sari suoi per non e sulle
clesiasti cr <713> no se interogato dal uescouo disa oue sola sistenza die si comisa rifacendo gli anco
intendere che quello che desideraria sapere da lui haria a da no suo poi che gia l haueu ucrra sicurato de
la uitana simplicem informa di testimonianza per altri otuto cio sto arciuescouo ha ancor ricusato di
rispondere a questo uescouo dicendo che egli era prima et di francia et che non poteua e sull esaminato da
che non ha giurisditione sopra di lui noni che seendosi alterato a sai il re sono uenuti a a li parenti die
so arciuescouo pregandomi di que sare con sua maesta che non uoglia fare pregiudicio a la dignita
cclesiastica et di suplicare il papa et se uno stricercato da sua maesta di come ella qualche uno la causa
sua non lo uoglia fare in alcuno uescouo di francia perche non si fida di alcuno di sei io deside

R51, Francia 22/30, Tours, 27 March 1589 decoded, not read

A count's gentleman brings news that two cavalry companies of Mayenne's (under Sagona and Farlandra, who was badly wounded) have been routed and their baggage taken. Orléans and Paris are in the League's hands, and Mayenne remains in Paris. The King wants peace, while Navarre is taking places.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

R52, Francia 22/31, Tours, 6 April 1589 decoded, not read

The castellan of Angers refuses the League's bribe, and Marshal d'Aumont secures the castle for the King, who writes him a letter in his own hand. The count is sent to Brittany against the Duke of Mercoeur; Alfonso Corso is in Auvergne; Épernon's command is disputed; Mayenne is still in Paris. The King keeps Holy Week and Easter devotions and touches for the scrofula.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

Transcription: DOC_R52_D1716_1716.txt

R57, Francia 22/36, n.d. (enclosures dated "dix di giugno" [1589], Moulins) decoded, not read

This record holds copies of papers sent to M. de la Guiche. The first says the King's Swiss army (about 12,000 Swiss and 2,000 lansquenets, some 17–18,000 men with 12 guns) is marching, paid with money lent by Strasbourg. The second is the oath of the inhabitants of Moulins to live and die Catholic and loyal to "Henri, by the grace of God King of France and Poland".

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

R58, Francia 22/37, Moulins, 5 July 1589 (dup.) decoded, not read

The King has taken Jargeau ("Giorgio") and its Loire bridge, hanged a captain and pardoned the governor. Other Loire towns have surrendered, and the King is heading for Paris, where four faubourgs are said to favour him. Mayenne has taken Montereau and Nemours is raising money at Lyon for the League's Swiss.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

goeia a questo s de le cose di qui io non la saro di dirle quello che s intende se bene con molta dificolta
si puo sapere una uerita poi che ognuno parla secondo il suo senso i e inteso che il re habia preso giorgio
hauendo quei di dentro aspetato il canone prima che uolersi rendere et perche il los no e deboli siano se
bene di m portanza per hauere un ponte di piestra sul fiume de la lo innato per es en lo so oho le leghe
lontano ad orliense dicono che sua maesta non habia uoluto acerear loro alcuna conditione ma liberam sua di
u tuttion totuto cio l ha preseruato dal sacro i senza molta dificolta et mala sodisfatione de soldati che
non e sendo pagati sperauano al feno di guadagna are qualcosa in quello ocasioneua a trata eoa o sua maesta
con i principali caualieri che sono seco ha fat <191> ira oricare un capitano che fu incolpato des erstato
quello che haueua persuasi i popoli a non si rendere et di farli a brusciare un borgo di un uom ni per
leuare la comodita da logiamentia l esercito oltre che questo capitano poco tempo prima haueuato chi danari
per fare gente in seruitio del reti i dapoi riceuti si trarriuoltato in fauore de la lega haueua anco sua
maesta conda nato ne la medesma pena il gouernatore di quello ch il man a condo to allogo del suplicio
hauendo diuersi signori interceso per lui con dire che era bon soldato et che ne la uenire hauria seruito a
sua maesta otentien per don roma pero si tiene tutauia prigione ha anco sua maesta u nato carcerare alcuni
de u principari del der a per hauu <500> e danari intende che quello digian era dieci leghe discorsi a da
giorgio et ric ire un altro ponte di piestra sopra il fiume de la loir a ueduto che il re sia presaria a
quello uolta haueuano inuiato a di mandar so corso al sig duca di rimen am non l hauendo hauto senza
aspetare l esercito haueano inuiato a rendersi a sua maesta come una no quasi tutelaltretere di quei
contorni che non si posono longamenire la quale li haueua amoreuolmi ceuti nel medesmo tempo dicono che il
nauanno haueua con ingano presto in lui uerse et risiera incaminata sua maesta che hora si ritroua padrona
di tuti i ponti che sono sopra la loir ad orliense on ante si pno ri et dicono che sia risoluta d andare di
ritan uerso parigi perator et dicesi che sono anco incaminati li suoi suizeri et di <037> ouo uengono
incontro ai quali haueua sua maesta inuiato il duca di longarii la ini scambio di mons de la fiissache era
prima stato destinato a questo carica et partito ancora con buona compagnia ma due giorni dopo hebe lettere
del re con le quali gli comandaua diritornarsene in questo parti del borboneste dicendo che presto gli
inuiar agente per fare la guerra i nouernia contra quello de la legai dice da altra i <022> nnte et il duca
di um uena haueua u preso montere o o anco castelo terit et sin caminaria per andare e a comba tere li
suizeri tenere prima che si post poi unire con sua maesta to et mons dine morire usaua ogni diligenza alione
s hauere danari per dare ai suizeri che uengono in seruitio de la legar neni quali diconoseere giuntiati am
beri in numero di fratici mili d uita buona gente la quale non uole pasare oltre se non le uengono dati
danari am et subiti haueis incaminarrano per la borgogna a la coda degl altri suizeri heretici per comba

e uere molto inanzi non hanoo a troua tantia per entrare in francia onde io non sar ueritta fermare a u s
illma sebene io ho parlato con uno del duca di di niuersche mi dice hauere lui medesmo la sato non so deti
suizerie nanto u s e gia molti giorni ma eglio a potria dire il falso in questo punto e uenuto a uederani
mons di unor gesche parti mercordi parsato da pluuiuersati doue dice che il medesmo giorno era anco partito
il re per et armi nessa uendo hauto auisi dal nauarro che i borghi di quello tera erano tu ti per sua maesta
to et gia haueuano anco guadagnata la forsa et piantata l artigliaria in tanto che sperauano molto presto di
hauere la tera dice anco che quatro dei borghi di parigi cuore san marc eosa nuitora nia che se et san
germano haueuano fare consapere a sua maesta che teniuano per lei et che uolendo andare a quello uolta che
loro l hauriano molto a le gran riceutato et per questo rispeto subito che sara terminata l impresa dieta
oue senza aspetare piu longa mi heranieri il re col suo esercito si incaminarra a quello uolta intendendosi
che il duca di dir hu menarra a me o si un poco a malato referisce ancora che il conte die oi sonetera
prigione nel castelo di nante si tra ha luato con metersi in una ci siane la qual soleuano portargli il
mangiare in castelo cosa asai dificile da credere non ho uoluto la sare di dire a u s illma quello che
questo mons mi ha refcrto se bene io gli presto poca fede e sendo molto parti al seruitore del re ma teso
certo che uiene da la corte hauendo animortate lettere del sig gale arao fregosto et de le nuoui si rimete a
quello che intendero da sto mons non mie parso douere la sare di darne conto a u s illma che le mere era in
quello consideratione et parera albingolar sua prudenza il papa sero io traso inna

R17, Francia 18/3, ff. 233r-235v, 1587 decoded, not read

A Morosini-period letter of 1587 in the Meister no. 40 key, filed by DECODE with the Bergamo/Nazareth despatches: it concerns the Duke of Lorraine and Guise, the German reiters, the Queen Mother and Schomberg.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

06 Sources

  • Archivio Apostolico Vaticano, Segreteria di Stato, Francia 22 (“Francia, card. Morosino, 1588 e 1589”), items 1–37: DECODE R18–R22, R27–R49, R51–R58. The images need a login; the digit transcriptions (by DECODE volunteers, 2016–17) and the clear transcriptions of R18/R19 are attached to the records.
  • A. Meister, Die Geheimschrift im Dienste der päpstlichen Kurie von ihren Anfängen bis zum Ende des XVI. Jahrhunderts (Paderborn 1906), pp. 393–394, no. 40, from the Argenti collection.
  • Archivio Apostolico Vaticano, Segreteria di Stato, Francia 18/3, ff. 233r–235v: DECODE R17, with the volunteer transcription D1611.
  • G. Lasry, reconstructed key “ASV – F22” on DECODE R18 (DOC_3175, October 2020). His second key, “ASV – F22b”, belongs to R53 and R54.
  • No printed edition of Morosini’s legation turned up (there is no Acta Nuntiaturae Gallicae volume for 1587–89); searched 19 September 2026.

Decoder, notes, the decoded pages and the summaries: targets/morosini1588/. Page images are not reproduced beyond the crop above.
